Its preposition-plus-suffix form binds direction and second-person address into one word, so the addressee commanded by {{ar:ٱتْلُ}} ({{tr:utlu}}) is also the recipient of what was revealed. The contrast with {{ar:مِن}} ({{tr:min}}) {{ar:دُونِهِۦ}} ({{tr:dūnihi}}) later is sharp: revelation comes to you, while refuge is denied apart from Him.\",\"root_display\":\"no lexical root\",\"root_gloss_range\":null,\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:إِلَيْكَ}} ({{tr:ilayka}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:5:semantic","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_777d32deda165f77ed7a","text":"{\"blocking_evidence\":null,\"headline\":\"lexical range\",\"reader_payoff\":\"The reader notices that the directional phrase makes the Prophet the receiver of revelation, not its author or source.\",\"reason\":\"The CRITICAL rows cohere with V4 lexical range and are narrowed to the local word sense.\",\"representative_source_ids\":[\"QS-27b3deaa\"],\"status\":\"used\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"focus_occurrence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:10:2","source_type":"qac_morpheme","support_id":"sup_7b9c0f23861be4c67653","text":"{\"lemma_ar\":\"كَلِمَٰت\",\"morph_features\":\"STEM|POS:N|LEM:kalima`t|ROOT:klm|FP|GEN\",\"morpheme_role\":\"STEM\",\"pos\":\"N\",\"qac_ref\":\"18:27:10:2\",\"qac_word_ref\":\"18:27:10\",\"root_ar\":\"ك ل م\",\"surface_ar\":\"كَلِمَٰتِ\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:17","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_7f2c2446f73232e287e0","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"possessed spatial/exclusion noun: the apart-from-Him domain where refuge is sought and denied\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:دُونِهِۦ}} ({{tr:dūnihi}}) is not a flat preposition by itself; it is a possessed spatial noun governed by {{ar:مِن}} ({{tr:min}}). The suffix makes the outside-domain definite relative to the same divine referent behind {{ar:رَبِّكَ}} ({{tr:rabbika}}) and {{ar:كَلِمَٰتِهِۦ}} ({{tr:kalimātihi}}). The root {{ar:د و ن}} ({{tr:d-w-n}}) layers below, apart from, and inferior to, so the failed search is directed into every lower or lesser domain outside Him. Because the exclusion phrase precedes the final refuge noun, the outside-Him domain is closed before the object appears; the common formula recurs at 18:58 and sharpens the prior denial of any protector in 18:26.\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:د و ن}} ({{tr:d-w-n}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"below, apart from, short of, and inferior to; local phrase makes a defined outside-domain relative to Him\",\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:دُونِهِۦ}} ({{tr:dūnihi}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:10","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_80666d77a531cb48d724","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"Form II active participle under categorical negation: no changer/alterer of His words\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:مُبَدِّلَ}} ({{tr:mubaddila}}) is the denied agent: an active participle whose force is completed by {{ar:لِ}} ({{tr:li}}) {{ar:كَلِمَٰتِهِۦ}} ({{tr:kalimātihi}}). The root {{ar:ب د ل}} ({{tr:b-d-l}}) ranges over changing, substituting, and replacing, but the local Form II participle emphasizes alteration agency; variant pressure toward wholesale replacement is a narrowed contrast, not the main parse. The formula echoes divine-word immutability passages such as 6:34, 6:115, and 10:64.\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:ب د ل}} ({{tr:b-d-l}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"change, exchange, replacement, and substitution; local Form II participle targets alteration agency\",\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:مُبَدِّلَ}} ({{tr:mubaddila}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:3","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_81326818300f4f03f3f2","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"relative pronoun functioning as the command object, open until the passive revelation clause defines it\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:مَآ}} ({{tr:mā}}) supplies the command object as \\\"that which,\\\" leaving the content open until {{ar:أُوحِىَ}} ({{tr:ūḥiya}}) and the source phrase define it. The long vowel also gives the object slot audible space between the clipped command and the passive revelation verb.\",\"root_display\":\"no lexical root\",\"root_gloss_range\":null,\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:مَآ}} ({{tr:mā}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:12:structure","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_820b83606f8b1cb65ef0","text":"{\"blocking_evidence\":null,\"headline\":\"local structure\",\"reader_payoff\":\"The reader notices protected words paired with denied refuge as the ayah moves from speech-domain to escape-domain.\",\"reason\":\"The CRITICAL rows cohere with the aligned QAC grammar and attachment constraints.\",\"representative_source_ids\":[\"QT-7e71d2d6\",\"QT-b37d3baa\",\"QB-26a7dbb4\",\"QB-ba20a79c\"],\"status\":\"used\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"18:27:8","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_86767b4c50bfd1a28d02","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"possessed singular Lord-title that makes the book source personally addressed\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:رَبِّكَ}} ({{tr:rabbika}}) completes the construct and makes the book source personally addressed: it is the book of your Lord.
